# Sex — Research

Skin: Sexcapades (`sexcapades.json`) · Domain: Intimacy, Eroticism, BDSM, Power Exchange & Relational Geometries · Meter: interest
Coverage: 382 topics, 730 positions (each give/receive direction counted as its own position; each multi-pole topic's poles counted individually; each single topic counted once), 36 archetypes (all counts taken from the skin JSON).

## 1. Domain framing

Sex maps consensual adult erotic practice: what people do, give, receive, wear, watch and imagine together, across power exchange, bondage, sensation, roleplay, sex acts and aftercare. The meter word is **interest**: the skin measures how into each practice a profile is, in each direction where direction exists. A position here is a rated stance on one practice or pole — being into it (positive), or averse to it (negative); on a single topic the topic itself is the one position, and a negative rating is aversion to that practice, not a missing answer. A 0 rating — no interest either way — is also a position. What does not count as a position is anything non-consensual or involving anyone who cannot consent: the skin's whole map presumes negotiation between adults. The rating contract: the inputs are advocacy per pole and openness per topic; receptivity is derived as round-half-even(openness × advocacy) computed with exact fractions, never rated directly, and intensity is the signed largest-|value| pole, likewise derived, never rated. On this action skin advocacy is expressed directionally — a give rating and a receive rating are separate positions and are never averaged. The Scribe is an indaba: it uses this research to show each position with its backing, name where holders meet and part, and ask what would let the group reach consensus. The skin's stance is descriptive and non-judgmental: it catalogues practices in their holders' own terms, treats giving and receiving as different experiences, and ranks nothing — a practice is on the map because people are into it, not because the skin endorses it.

## 2. Topic rationale

**BDSM archetypes** — It is on the map because authority, obedience and negotiated control has followers for whom this specific practice, not the broader category, is the point. Its poles are rated independently and need not be opposites: Sovereign (Dominant / Master) — Order, authority, structure, law, absolute responsibility. Daddy/Mommy — Safety, containment, emotional holding, loving guidance. Primal (Hunter) — Raw animalistic chase, wrestling, growling, uninhibited drive. Sadist (Tormentor / Sensual) — Endorphin generation, aesthetic pain infliction, erotic torment. Brat (Rebel / Provoker) — Playful boundary-testing, sassy pushback, earning punishment. Switch (Fluid / Versatile) — Dynamic role-reversal, intuitive polarity swapping. Submissive (Slave / Devotee) — Complete somatic surrender, service, egoless offering. Masochist — Aroused by receiving pain, intensity, or suffering inside a negotiated scene. Primal (Prey) — Pursued, caught, overpowered in a negotiated animalistic frame — the receiving side of a primal scene. Brat-tamer — Meets a brat's poking with structure, consequence, and play, without breaking the negotiation. The topic's stated question is: The ten poles: Sovereign, Daddy/Mommy, Primal (Hunter), Sadist, Brat, Switch, Submissive, Masochist, Primal (Prey), and Brat-tamer.

The topic's stated question is: Which sensory channel: tactile, thermal, impact, electric, olfactory, acoustic, or vestibular.

## 4. Archetype research

### Sadist — `images/emblems/sadist.svg`
Enjoys giving intense sensation — including pain — in consensual play. A sadist gets pleasure from their partner's reactions, and scenes are negotiated in advance with clear limits and consent. Why it holds its positions, tied to §3: its strongest ratings follow the §3 reasons for the practices it rates highest — giving side: Sadomasochism, Impact Play, OTK Spanking; receiving side: Praise (aftercare) — where holders name the partner's reaction, surrendered control, or the specific sensation itself as the draw. Receptive to: neighbouring practices in the same families as Sadomasochism, Impact Play, OTK Spanking, because the §3 reasons transfer — a partner who shares the underlying draw (sensation, control, service, exposure or care) can usually negotiate the specific form. Closed to: Waterboarding, Knife Play, Ballbusting, where its ratings are negative — on this skin a negative is aversion in the holder's own terms, most often because the direction is wrong for them (they give what they will not receive, or the reverse) or the practice's core sensation or dynamic is simply not their draw. Emblem: images/emblems/sadist.svg

### Masochist — `images/emblems/masochist.svg`
Enjoys receiving intense sensation — including pain — in consensual play. A masochist finds pleasure, release, or connection in surrendering to sensation within agreed limits. Why it holds its positions, tied to §3: its strongest ratings follow the §3 reasons for the practices it rates highest — giving side: Cuddling, Affection, Next-day check-in; receiving side: Sadomasochism, Impact Play, Extreme endurance — where holders name the partner's reaction, surrendered control, or the specific sensation itself as the draw. Receptive to: neighbouring practices in the same families as Cuddling, Affection, Next-day check-in, because the §3 reasons transfer — a partner who shares the underlying draw (sensation, control, service, exposure or care) can usually negotiate the specific form. Closed to: Waterboarding, Knife Play, Cock and Ball Torture, where its ratings are negative — on this skin a negative is aversion in the holder's own terms, most often because the direction is wrong for them (they give what they will not receive, or the reverse) or the practice's core sensation or dynamic is simply not their draw. Emblem: images/emblems/masochist.svg

### Rope Bottom — `images/emblems/rope-bunny.svg`
Enjoys being tied. The appeal can be the restraint, the rope itself, or the trust of the tie. The community also says rope bunny. Being a rope bottom does not make someone a submissive. Why it holds its positions, tied to §3: its strongest ratings follow the §3 reasons for the practices it rates highest — giving side: Trust, Anticipation, Spontaneous sex; receiving side: Armbinders, Spreaders, Predicament Bondage — where holders name the partner's reaction, surrendered control, or the specific sensation itself as the draw. Receptive to: neighbouring practices in the same families as Trust, Anticipation, Spontaneous sex, because the §3 reasons transfer — a partner who shares the underlying draw (sensation, control, service, exposure or care) can usually negotiate the specific form. Closed to: Anal hooks, Crucifixion Play, Kidnapping, where its ratings are negative — on this skin a negative is aversion in the holder's own terms, most often because the direction is wrong for them (they give what they will not receive, or the reverse) or the practice's core sensation or dynamic is simply not their draw. Emblem: images/emblems/rope-bunny.svg

### Rigger — `images/emblems/rigger.svg`
Enjoys tying a partner up with rope, often in Shibari-style bondage. A rigger focuses on technique, safety, and the shared experience of restraint, treating rope as both craft and connection. Why it holds its positions, tied to §3: its strongest ratings follow the §3 reasons for the practices it rates highest — giving side: Predicament Bondage, Armbinders, Trust; receiving side: Leather — where holders name the partner's reaction, surrendered control, or the specific sensation itself as the draw. Receptive to: neighbouring practices in the same families as Predicament Bondage, Armbinders, Trust, because the §3 reasons transfer — a partner who shares the underlying draw (sensation, control, service, exposure or care) can usually negotiate the specific form. Closed to: Kidnapping, Self-bondage, Waterbondage, where its ratings are negative — on this skin a negative is aversion in the holder's own terms, most often because the direction is wrong for them (they give what they will not receive, or the reverse) or the practice's core sensation or dynamic is simply not their draw. Closed to: Siblings, Cuckold Humiliation, Degradation, where its ratings are negative — on this skin a negative is aversion in the holder's own terms, most often because the direction is wrong for them (they give what they will not receive, or the reverse) or the practice's core sensation or dynamic is simply not their draw. Emblem: images/emblems/stag-vixen.svg

## 5. Gap analysis

**Positions with no archetype.** The 36 archetypes are role identities (Sadist, Rigger, Little, Bull, and so on), so most of the map's 730 positions have no archetype embodying them as an identity: whole families — positions, most clothing and materials topics, most fluids and food play, most fantasy and transformation scenarios — are practices people rate without any archetype being *about* them. Archetype coverage is also partial in the data itself: only 15 of the 38 samples rate all 382 topics; the 36 archetypes rate between 34 and 382 topics each, so many archetype-by-topic combinations simply have no rating to research (see the JSON note below).

**Thin topics.** Every single-pole topic is thin by design in the same way: it registers affinity or aversion to one practice, with no contrasting pole, so the indaba can list its backing (§3) but cannot locate a meeting point on it — this is a valid affinity shape, noted here as thin, not as an error. The multi-pole radar topics (up to ten poles) are the opposite: broad, but each pole's backing is necessarily brief. Directional topics are thin in a second sense: the JSON supplies only a name and give/receive labels, with no question or descriptive text, so §2 and §3 reconstruct their rationale from the name and category alone.

**Skin-JSON problems (reported, not fixed).** (1) The top-level `about` field is empty, and `domain` carries the framing instead. (2) 360 of 382 topics have no `question` field; only the 22 multi-pole topics carry both a `question` and a `hovertip`, whose `issue` then serves as the de facto question whose `issue` then serves as the de facto question — the remaining topics have neither, so the topic name is the whole specification. (3) Archetype and sample `count` values and rating coverage vary widely (34–382 ratings per profile; see above), so `count` cannot be read as 'rates the whole skin' except for the 15 full-coverage samples. (4) Many topics carry an unexplained `kinksters` number whose source and meaning are not stated in the JSON; this research does not use it as a statistic. (5) Two samples (Adam Peasant, Eve Vale) are kind "sample", not archetypes, and are therefore covered as profiles in the skin but have no §4 entry of their own.

**Deliberately left out of this research.** Anything non-consensual, anything involving minors or animals as sexual participants (animal *roleplay* between adults is a topic in the skin and is covered as such), and any medical or safety instruction: §3 gives holders' reasons, not how-to guidance, and risk-flagged practices are described, not taught. No statistic is reported except the one listed in §6; for the great majority of individual practices no reliable prevalence figure exists, and §3 says so rather than estimating.


## 6. Sources

Statistics used in §3 (name + year + where to find it):
- Richters, J. et al. (2008), "Demographic and psychosocial features of participants in bondage and discipline, 'sadomasochism' or dominance and submission (BDSM): data from a national survey", *Journal of Sexual Medicine* — the Australian Study of Health and Relationships probability sample; past-year BDSM participation 2.2% of men and 1.3% of women. Used only for the Sadist and Masochist poles of the BDSM archetypes topic, the one place a population figure for the named practice exists.

Grounding for reasons (traditions and texts drawn on, not statistics): the skin JSON's own topic names, give/receive labels, hovertip texts and archetype `about` texts, which are the holders'-terms vocabulary §2–§4 follow; and the widely shared community framing those texts themselves use — negotiation, consent, direction (give/receive) and aftercare — as stated in the skin's own archetype descriptions. No other sources were used; no source above is cited for a statistic it did not supply.
